❯ /plan I want to create a repository in this directory and push it to github,  
name we will decide after scoping out the initial plan. This is the problem I   
want to solve: companies and organizations have to make decisions about how     
they will integrate AI in to their systems, processes, and organizations. They  
may have the opportunity to use frontier tools, or may be limited to            
non-frontier tools like Gemini, kimi, or others. Deciding on a model is         
difficult and I want to reduce the complexity of that by implementing a custom  
benchmark tool, essentially allowing you to evaluate how well an agent can      
replicate existing features, environments, applications in a company's          
codebase, and how well it can implement new features or applications in a       
company's codebase, specifically feeding the benchmark tool with the code you   
want to to test against and/or new features you want to test how well an agent  
can add. An example is the company wants to know how well an LLM can create     
staging and test environments using context from existing production and        
development environments. Will it catch all dependencies, how accurately will   
it do it? For new features, lets say the company wants to add an analytics      
dashboard from internal data (with no prior context or similar applications in  
its codebase). again, how well will an agent do that? I don't anticipate this   
tool to have UI, ideally I'd want it to be pure CLI/algorithmic. I also want to 
look at popular open source benchmarking tools to compare how they work and     
take a similar approach, I don't want to reinvent the wheel. I don't want a     
proof of concept, I want something that is robust and fully fuctional. As you   
develop, create issues in the github repo, add github actions, make it open     
source with Apache 2.0, create thorough documentation and architecture          
diagrams, and look at the burnwise repo in this directory to see how it         
leverages github actions/dependabot/github security so we arent pushing to main 
and ensuring that all checks pass before merges to main. This should be         
self-hosted.

⏺ Updated plan
  ⎿  /plan to preview

⏺ The plan is written. Here's the summary before you approve.

  reforge — what we're building

  A self-hosted, Docker-based, Python CLI (no UI) that benchmarks how well any
  AI coding agent performs against your own codebase, on the two decisions that
  actually drive adoption:

  1. Replication — reproduce existing features/environments from codebase
  context (e.g. staging/test from prod), scored on did it catch every 
  dependency, and how accurately.
  2. New-feature — build net-new functionality with no prior context (e.g. an
  analytics dashboard).

  Key architectural bets (all validated against SWE-bench + Terminal-bench)

  - Task = a directory (task.yaml + Dockerfile + verifier/ + gold/), one unified
  schema with a category field — Terminal-bench's bundle pattern.
  - BYO-agent adapter ABC discovered via entry points; harness owns the
  container, adapter just drives its agent inside /workspace; harness captures 
  the diff via git so all adapters are uniform. Ships noop, gold, api_agent,
  claude-code, aider.
  - Hybrid scoring: deterministic tests (SWE-bench F2P/P2P, parsed from
  junitxml/jest-json/etc.) + a novel dependency-coverage scorer that emits an
  explicit missed list + a weighted LLM-judge rubric. Reports both a continuous
  final_score and a binary resolved.
  - Gold-patch self-verification as the keystone test (a task is only valid if
  its oracle passes) — runs in CI with no LLM cost.
  - GitHub hardening ported from burnwise: protected main, CI/CodeQL/Trivy
  gates, Dependabot, issue/PR templates — adapted Node→Python
  (ruff/mypy/pytest).

  Delivery

  Six phases as GitHub issues on feature-branch PRs. Phase 1 is a real 
  end-to-end vertical slice (not a POC): verify-gold passes and noop fails on a
  tiny task, in CI with Docker, before we build out real agents and the full
  scorer.

  One thing to flag: this is a large, multi-phase build. My intent on approval
  is to start at Phase 0 (repo bootstrap + full .github/) and Phase 1 (keystone 
  slice), get CI green, then proceed through the later phases — creating GitHub
  issues as we go. I'll need you to handle the gh auth/GitHub remote creation
  step interactively (I'll prompt you with a ! command when we get there).

  ⎿  Plan saved to: ~/.claude/plans/idempotent-moseying-lemur.md · /plan to edit
     reforge — AI Coding-Agent Benchmark for Your Own Codebase

     Context

     Companies and organizations must decide which AI coding agent/model to 
     adopt — frontier tools
     (Claude, GPT) or non-frontier ones (Gemini, Kimi, etc.). That decision is
     hard because the honest
     question isn't "how does model X score on public benchmarks" but "how well
     does agent X do work
     against our own codebase." Two capabilities matter most:

     1. Replication — reproduce existing features/environments/applications
     using context already in
     the codebase (e.g. "create staging + test environments mirroring prod/dev":
     did it catch all
     dependencies? how accurately?).
     2. New-feature implementation — build net-new functionality with little/no
     prior context
     (e.g. "add an analytics dashboard from internal events data").

     reforge is a self-hosted, Docker-based, model-agnostic CLI (no UI, pure CLI
     + library, Python)
     that runs a company's own tasks against any agent and produces
     reproducible, comparable scores. It
     mirrors proven patterns from SWE-bench (task = repo@commit + problem + gold
     patch +
     FAIL_TO_PASS/PASS_TO_PASS, containerized harness, gold self-verification,
     results JSON) and
     Terminal-bench (task-bundle-as-directory with Dockerfile + verifier +
     oracle; strict
     agent/harness separation), and adds two things the problem needs that
     neither has: a
     dependency-coverage scorer and a unified two-category task schema.

     Locked decisions: Python · BYO-agent adapter interface (+ reference
     adapters) · hybrid scoring
     (deterministic tests + dependency-coverage + LLM-judge rubric) · both task
     categories in v1 ·
     self-hosted, Apache 2.0 · GitHub with protected main, Dependabot,
     CodeQL/Trivy, CI gates
     (patterned on the Burnwise/burnwise repo).

     Reference patterns reused (do not reinvent)

     - SWE-bench containerized eval + F2P/P2P resolved criterion + gold-patch
     self-verification.
     - Terminal-bench task bundle (dir with Dockerfile + verifier + oracle) +
     agent/adapter split.
     - Burnwise GitHub setup
     (/Users/filip/Documents/Personal/Burnwise/burnwise/.github/): reuse its
     codeql.yml, security.yml (Trivy SARIF + fixable CRITICAL/HIGH gate),
     dependabot.yml
     (npm→pip, docker, github-actions), issue/PR templates, config.yml,
     SECURITY.md, release.yml,
     CONTRIBUTING.md, CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md (Contributor Covenant 2.1), Makefile +
     .gitignore style.
     Adapt Node→Python (ruff/mypy/pytest instead of eslint/tsc/playwright).

     Package layout (src/ layout, package reforge, CLI reforge)

     reforge/
     ├── pyproject.toml (hatchling, PEP 621, entry points) · LICENSE (Apache
     2.0) · NOTICE · README.md
     ├── CONTRIBUTING.md · CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md · SECURITY.md · Makefile ·
     .gitignore · .pre-commit-config.yaml
     ├── .github/{workflows/{ci.yml,codeql.yml,security.yml,release.yml},dependa
     bot.yml,
     │
     ISSUE_TEMPLATE/*,pull_request_template.md,config.yml,CODEOWNERS}
     ├── docs/ (architecture.md + Mermaid diagrams, task-authoring.md,
     adapter-authoring.md)
     ├── src/reforge/
     │   ├── cli.py            # Typer app, thin — delegates to services
     │   ├── config.py         # pydantic-settings: registries, dirs, secrets
     │   ├── spec/             # models.py (pydantic v2 TaskSpec), loader.py,
     validate.py, schema.py (JSON Schema export)
     │   ├── dataset/          # loader.py (local dir + HF), registry.py
     │   ├── workspace/        # source.py (git@sha|local|tarball via subprocess
     git), prepare.py (copy, strip_git, hidden_paths)
     │   ├── runtime/          # base.py (ContainerRuntime ABC +
     ContainerHandle), docker_runtime.py, image.py, limits.py
     │   ├── adapters/         # base.py (AgentAdapter ABC), registry.py (entry
     points), process.py, claude_code.py, aider.py, api_agent.py, noop.py,
     gold.py
     │   ├── runner/           # orchestrator.py (ThreadPoolExecutor),
     task_runner.py (per-task lifecycle), artifacts.py, run_context.py
     │   ├── scoring/          # base.py (Scorer ABC), tests.py, log_parsers.py,
     dependency.py, judge.py, compose.py
     │   ├── report/           # models.py, aggregate.py, render.py
     (rich/markdown/json)
     │   ├── llm/              # client.py (provider-agnostic + rate limiter),
     cost.py (pricing table)
     │   └── utils/            # logging.py (structlog), hashing.py, errors.py
     ├── tasks/                # sample-replication-*, sample-newfeature-*
     (shipped dataset)
     └── tests/{unit,integration,fixtures/tiny-task}

     Task specification (on-disk task directory, Terminal-bench style)

     tasks/<id>/
     ├── task.yaml        # TaskSpec (pydantic-validated)
     ├── Dockerfile       # environment (pin base image by digest)
     ├── verifier/        # run_tests.sh + held-out tests — injected AFTER diff
     capture
     ├── gold/solution.patch  # oracle for self-verification
     └── rubric.yaml      # optional (or inline in task.yaml)
     Unified schema (single category: replication | new_feature field), key
     blocks:
     - source (git repo@SHA | local | tarball; strip_git; subdir)
     - environment (dockerfile, build_args, workdir)
     - instruction (the prompt) · context (visible/hidden globs)
     - verification (entrypoint, framework, fail_to_pass, pass_to_pass, timeout)
     - dependency_coverage (required.{services,config_refs,imports} ground truth
     + detectors[])
     - rubric (weighted criteria, scale, judge prompts)
     - scoring.weights {tests, dependency_coverage, judge} +
     gate.require_pass_to_pass
     - resources (cpus, memory, pids, network=none default, timeouts,
     disk_quota)

     Replication tasks lean on dependency_coverage.required + rubric;
     new-feature tasks lean on
     fail_to_pass tests + rubric. Ship one worked example of each in tasks/.

     Agent adapter contract (adapters/base.py)

     Thin, I/O-oriented; harness owns the container, adapter drives its agent
     inside it.
     - AdapterInput(instruction, workspace_path, container: ContainerHandle, 
     model, config, env, logger, timeout_s)
     - AdapterResult(success, trace_path, token_usage, cost_usd, exit_code, 
     metadata, error)
     - AgentAdapter.validate(input) (fail fast) + run(input) (modify files in
     /workspace, produce trace).
     - Harness captures the diff (git diff vs a pre-agent commit inside the
     container) — uniform across
     all adapters; adapter never computes scores or reports the diff.
     - Discovery via entry points ([project.entry-points."reforge.adapters"]) →
     third-party pip install.
     - Reference adapters: noop + gold (no LLM, for testing), api_agent
     (Anthropic/OpenAI/Gemini
     mini-loop), claude-code, aider.

     Per-task lifecycle (runner/task_runner.py, SWE-bench-style)

     resolve source → prep workspace (strip_git, hidden_paths; source only, no 
     tests/gold) → build/pull
     image (cache key = hash(Dockerfile+args), serialized builds) → start
     container (resource limits,
     --network none, --cap-drop ALL, no docker socket) → snapshot pre-agent
     commit → adapter.run
     (agent_timeout, SIGKILL) → capture prediction.patch → inject verifier + 
     held-out tests → run
     scorers → compose → persist
     runs/<run-id>/tasks/<id>/{prediction.patch,test.log,dep_report.json, 
     judge.json,result.json,agent_trace.log} → cleanup container. Orchestrator =
     bounded
     ThreadPoolExecutor (--concurrency), image-build lock, API rate-limiter,
     run.json provenance
     (tool/adapter versions, image digests, source SHA).

     Hybrid scoring (scoring/)

     - tests.py — exec verifier, parse machine formats (pytest junitxml, jest
     --json, go test -json,
     JUnit XML) → F2P = fraction of fail_to_pass now passing; P2P boolean;
     resolved = F2P==1 ∧ P2P.
     - dependency.py — detectors (python_imports via AST, env_refs,
     terraform_refs,
     service_deps from compose/k8s, grep fallback; registry-extensible) →
     coverage = |required∩found|/|required|,
     and an explicit missed list (the most actionable buyer output).
     - judge.py — structured-JSON rubric scoring, temperature 0, --judge-samples
     median, anti-reward-hacking
     prompt, records model id + prompt hash; weighted, never hard-gates.
     - compose.py — final = Σ weight·subscore; regressions (P2P fail) → final = 
     0; report both
     continuous final_score and binary resolved. Per-task result.json +
     aggregate leaderboard
     (resolved_rate, mean_final_score, by_category, mean_dep_coverage, cost,
     duration).

     CLI surface (Typer)

     reforge init <id> [--category] · reforge validate <dir|--dataset> ·
     reforge verify-gold <dir> (apply gold, assert resolved — CI gate) ·
     reforge run --dataset --adapter --model [--run-id --concurrency --task-id 
     --judge-model --judge-samples --no-judge --network --max-cost-usd --repeats
     --no-cache --dry-run] ·
     reforge report <run-dir> [--format table|markdown|json --leaderboard 
     --compare RUN...] ·
     reforge list adapters|datasets|detectors · reforge adapter-check --adapter 
     --model.

     Dependencies (lean; LLM/HF isolated as extras)

     Core: pydantic+pydantic-settings, typer, rich, docker (SDK), pyyaml,
     structlog; git via
     subprocess (no GitPython). Extras:
     reforge[judge]→anthropic,openai,google-genai;
     reforge[hf]→datasets. Dev: pytest+pytest-mock, ruff, mypy, hatchling.

     GitHub / CI / security (self-hosted, protected main)

     - Branch protection on main: require PR + passing checks (ci, codeql,
     security), no direct pushes,
     linear history; CODEOWNERS.
     - ci.yml: ruff → mypy → unit tests → Docker job (gold self-verify +
     noop/gold adapters); LLM-judge
     mocked (no API cost in CI). codeql.yml (python, weekly). security.yml
     (Trivy SARIF + fixable
     CRITICAL/HIGH gate). release.yml (tag→GitHub release, validate
     tag==version). dependabot.yml
     (pip + github-actions, weekly, grouped). Issue/PR templates, config.yml,
     SECURITY.md,
     CONTRIBUTING.md, CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md, .pre-commit-config.yaml.
     - Create GitHub issues per phase item below; do work on feature branches →
     PRs into main.

     Security / risk mitigations

     Untrusted agent output: one container/task, --network none default,
     --cap-drop ALL, pids/mem/cpu
     limits, disk quota, hard timeouts, workspace is a copy, no docker-socket
     mount — document
     "run on disposable/isolated hosts." Reward hacking: tests/gold injected
     after diff capture; P2P
     regression gate; judge detects hardcoding; optional held-out-test hash
     check. Reproducibility: pin
     SHAs + image digests + versions in run.json; --no-judge for fully
     deterministic runs.
     Cost: per-task/run accounting + --max-cost-usd abort. Flakiness: --repeats
     + variance in report.

     Phased delivery (→ GitHub issues, feature-branch PRs)

     - Phase 0 — Bootstrap: repo, pyproject/src-layout, Apache 2.0
     LICENSE+NOTICE, ruff/mypy/pytest,
     full .github/ (CI/CodeQL/Trivy/Dependabot/templates), branch protection,
     README vision, docs skeleton.
     - Phase 1 — Keystone vertical slice (must be real end-to-end): spec
     (new_feature, local source),
     docker_runtime, task_runner, tests scorer (junitxml), compose (tests-only),
     noop+gold adapters,
     validate/verify-gold/run --task-id/report --json, tests/fixtures/tiny-task.
     Exit:
     verify-gold passes and noop fails on tiny-task, in CI with Docker.
     - Phase 2 — Real agents + orchestration: full adapter contract +
     entry-point registry + process.py;
     api_agent, claude-code, aider; orchestrator (concurrency, build lock,
     provenance); git source +
     strip_git; run --dataset, list adapters, adapter-check; rich progress +
     table/markdown reports.
     - Phase 3 — Full hybrid scoring + replication: dependency scorer + detector
     registry; judge harness
       - sampling/median + cost accounting + rate limiter; full compose/gating +
     schemas; replication category
     end-to-end + sample replication task; more log parsers (jest, go test,
     JUnit).
     - Phase 4 — Scale/hardening: HF dataset loader ([hf]); report --compare
     leaderboard;
     --max-cost-usd/--repeats/variance; security hardening pass; docs site +
     architecture diagrams +
     authoring guides + published JSON Schema.
     - Phase 5 — Ecosystem: company-agnostic sample task cookbook; third-party
     adapter plugin template;
     public leaderboard format.

     Subagents as stand-in test-subject LLMs (development-time)

     To exercise the full reforge run pipeline live during Phases 1–2 without 
     needing external agent
     binaries or API keys, use spawned Claude Code subagents as the "agent under
     test":
     - Add a dev/reference adapter (e.g. adapters/subagent.py, or drive the
     existing api_agent via a
     subagent) that receives the task instruction + workspace and returns file
     edits — letting me run a
     real end-to-end benchmark against a live LLM through the harness while
     building it.
     - Use subagents to author and stress-test the sample tasks (generate
     candidate solutions, confirm
     fail_to_pass really fails without the fix and passes with it, sanity-check
     rubric wording).
     - This validates orchestration, diff capture, verifier injection, and all
     three scorers end-to-end
     before wiring real frontier/non-frontier adapters. (Note: the subagent
     adapter is a development/test
     convenience, not a shipped product dependency — the shipped path is the
     entry-point adapters.)

     Documentation authored with the humanizer skill

     Run every human-facing doc through the humanizer skill so it doesn't read
     as AI-generated:
     README, CONTRIBUTING, CODE_OF_CONDUCT, SECURITY, docs/architecture.md,
     docs/task-authoring.md,
     docs/adapter-authoring.md, and issue/PR templates. Apply after drafting
     each doc, before commit.
     Architecture diagrams (Mermaid) accompany the humanized prose.

     Verification

     - Unit: spec round-trips + JSON Schema; log parsers on captured fixtures;
     dependency detectors;
     compose math + regression gating; judge normalization (mocked LLM);
     registry/limits/aggregate.
     - Integration (Docker-marked): gold-patch self-verification on tiny-task
     and every shipped task
     (resolved == true); noop adapter → resolved == false, F2P==0; concurrency
     isolation smoke.
     - Live E2E via subagents: run reforge run on the sample tasks using a
     subagent-backed adapter
     as the test-subject LLM — confirms the full pipeline and produces a real
     leaderboard row.
     - Manual E2E: reforge run --dataset ./tasks --adapter api_agent --model 
     <frontier> --run-id smoke
     then --model <non-frontier>, and reforge report --compare to confirm
     cross-model leaderboard.
     - CI green on ruff+mypy+unit+Docker gold-verify before any merge to main.

  ⎿  Added 18 lines, removed 4 lines
       79  def match_status(expected_id: str, results: list[TestCaseResul
           t]) -> TestStatus | None:
       80      """Find the status for a task-declared test id, tolerating
            id-format drift.
       81  
       82 -    Tries exact match first, then suffix matches in both direc
          -tions so that, for                                            
       83 -    example, ``tests/test_x.py::test_y`` still matches when th
          -e runner reports a                                            
       84 -    slightly different rootdir-relative path.                 
       82 +    Frameworks report node ids inconsistently: pytest's JUnit 
          +output uses a                                                 
       83 +    dotted module classname with no ``.py`` (``test_calc::test
          +_add``), while task                                           
       84 +    authors naturally write path-style ids (``test_calc.py::te
          +st_add``). We                                                 
       85 +    normalize both to a dotted, extension-free form and compar
          +e exactly, then by                                            
       86 +    suffix (so a rootdir prefix on either side still matches).
       87      """
       88      by_id = {r.nodeid: r.status for r in results}
       89      if expected_id in by_id:
       90          return by_id[expected_id]
       91  
       92 +    want = _normalize_id(expected_id)                         
       93      for r in results:
       91 -        if r.nodeid.endswith(expected_id) or expected_id.endsw
          -ith(r.nodeid):                                                
       94 +        have = _normalize_id(r.nodeid)                        
       95 +        if want == have or have.endswith("." + want) or want.e
          +ndswith("." + have):                                          
       96              return r.status
       97      return None
       98 +                                                              
       99 +                                                              
      100 +def _normalize_id(node_id: str) -> str:                       
      101 +    """Collapse ``path/mod.py::Class::test`` and ``mod.Class.t
          +est`` to one form."""                                         
      102 +    return (                                                  
      103 +        node_id.replace(".py", "")                            
      104 +        .replace("::", ".")                                   
      105 +        .replace("/", ".")                                    
      106 +        .strip(".")                                           
      107 +    )

       80 +class _Progress:                                              
       81 +    """A thin rich progress bar that no-ops when disabled or o
          +utput isn't a tty."""                                         
       82 +                                                              
       83 +    def __init__(self, total: int, *, enabled: bool) -> None: 
       84 +        self._enabled = enabled                               
       85 +        self._total = total                                   
       86 +        self._progress = None                                 
       87 +        self._task_id = None                                  
       88 +        self._done = 0                                        
       89 +        self._resolved = 0                                    
       90 +                                                              
       91 +    def __enter__(self) -> _Progress:                         
       92 +        if self._enabled:                                     
       93 +            from rich.progress import BarColumn, MofNCompleteC
          +olumn, Progress, TextColumn                                   
       94 +                                                              
       95 +            self._progress = Progress(                        
       96 +                TextColumn("[bold]running[/bold]"),           
       97 +                BarColumn(),                                  
       98 +                MofNCompleteColumn(),                         
       99 +                TextColumn("{task.fields[status]}"),          
      100 +            )                                                 
      101 +            self._progress.start()                            
      102 +            self._task_id = self._progress.add_task("run", tot
          +al=self._total, status="")                                    
      103 +        return self                                           
      104 +                                                              
      105 +    def advance(self, result: TaskResult) -> None:            
      106 +        self._done += 1                                       
      107 +        if result.resolved:                                   
      108 +            self._resolved += 1                               
      109 +        if self._progress is not None:                        
      110 +            self._progress.update(                            
      111 +                self._task_id,                                
      112 +                advance=1,                                    
      113 +                status=f"[green]{self._resolved} resolved[/gre
          +en]",                                                         
      114 +            )                                                 
      115 +                                                              
      116 +    def __exit__(self, *exc: object) -> None:                 
      117 +        if self._progress is not None:                        
      118 +            self._progress.stop()

      229 +    """Preflight an adapter's credentials/config without runni
          +ng a task.                                                    
      230 +                                                              
      231 +    Resolves the adapter and runs its validate() against a lig
          +htweight input, so                                            
      232 +    you catch a missing API key or unknown model before spendi
          +ng a full run.                                                
      233 +    """                                                       
      234 +    from reforge.adapters.base import AdapterInput            
      235 +    from reforge.adapters.registry import load_adapter        
      236 +                                                              
      237 +    try:                                                      
      238 +        instance = load_adapter(adapter)                      
      239 +        probe = AdapterInput(                                 
      240 +            instruction="preflight",                          
      241 +            workspace_path="/workspace",                      
      242 +            container=_NullContainer(),  # type: ignore[arg-ty
          +pe]                                                           
      243 +            trace_path=Path("/dev/null"),                     
      244 +            model=model,                                      
      245 +            config=_parse_config(config),                     
      246 +        )                                                     
      247 +        instance.validate(probe)                              
      248 +    except ReforgeError as exc:                               
      249 +        _fail(str(exc))                                       
      250 +        return                                                
      251 +    console.print(f"[green]✓ {adapter} looks ready[/green] (v{
          +instance.version})")                                          
      252 +                                                              
      253 +

      256      """List installed agent adapters."""
     ...

           m]")

      289 +def _parse_config(config: str | None) -> dict[str, object]:   
      290 +    if not config:                                            
      291 +        return {}                                             
      292 +    try:                                                      
      293 +        parsed = json.loads(config)                           
      294 +    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:                       
      295 +        _fail(f"--config is not valid JSON: {exc}")           
      296 +        return {}                                             
      297 +    if not isinstance(parsed, dict):                          
      298 +        _fail("--config must be a JSON object")               
      299 +        return {}                                             
      300 +    return parsed                                             
      301 +                                                              
      302 +                                                              
      303  def _default_run_id(adapter: str) -> str:
      304      stamp = datetime.now(UTC).strftime("%Y%m%d-%H%M%S")
      305      return f"{adapter}-{stamp}"

       99  
      100  ## Bringing your own agent
      101  
      102 -An adapter is a small class that drives one agent inside the c
          -ontainer. reforge                                             
      103 -ships `noop` and `gold` (no LLM, for testing the harness) and,
          - as of the agent                                              
      104 -milestone, reference adapters for API models and popular agent
          - CLIs. Adapters are                                           
      105 -discovered through Python entry points, so a third-party adapt
          -er is just a package

      102 +An adapter is a small class that drives one agent inside the c
          +ontainer. The ones                                            
      103 +that ship today:                                              
      104  
      105 +- `noop` and `gold`: no LLM, used to test the harness itself. 
      106 +- `api-agent`: a minimal tool-loop agent over any model reacha
          +ble through the                                               
      107 +  Anthropic API or an OpenAI-compatible endpoint (OpenAI, Gemi
          +ni, Kimi, and so                                              
      108 +  on). This is how you compare a frontier model against a non-
          +frontier one on the                                           
      109 +  same scaffold.                                              
      110 +- `claude-code` and `aider`: thin wrappers around those CLIs r
          +unning inside the                                             
      111 +  task container.                                             
      112 +- `command`: run any shell command as the agent. The task inst
          +ruction and model                                             
      113 +  are passed in as environment variables, so you can wire up a
          + tool that has no                                             
      114 +  Python adapter at all.                                      
      115 +                                                              
      116 +Adapters are discovered through Python entry points, so a thir
          +d-party adapter is                                            
      117 +just a package you `pip install`. `reforge list adapters` show
          +s what's installed,                                           
      118 +and `reforge adapter-check --adapter <name>` preflights its cr
          +edentials. See                                                
      119 +[docs/adapter-authoring.md](docs/adapter-authoring.md).       
      120 +                                                              
      121 +Pick the model with `--model`, and pass adapter-specific optio
          +ns as JSON with                                               
      122 +`--config`, for example:                                      
      123 +                                                              
      124 +```bash                                                       
      125 +reforge run --task tests/fixtures/tiny-task --adapter api-agen
          +t --model claude-sonnet-4-6                                   
      126 +reforge run --task my-task --adapter command \                
      127 +  --config '{"command": "my-agent --model $REFORGE_MODEL \"$RE
          +FORGE_INSTRUCTION\""}'                                        
      128 +```                                                           
      129 +

      131  
      132  reforge runs code produced by an agent. Each task runs in its

⏺ Three phases shipped and merged with green CI. Here's where reforge stands.

  Live at github.com/filipjevtic/reforge

  Shipped & merged to protected main (3 PRs, all checks green):
  - Phase 0 — repo bootstrap: Apache 2.0, src-layout package, ruff/mypy/pytest
  (ruff pinned to 0.16.0), full .github/ (CI, CodeQL, Trivy, Dependabot,
  issue/PR templates, CODEOWNERS), README + architecture/authoring docs (Mermaid
  diagrams, run through the humanizer skill — zero em-dashes), branch
  protection requiring PR + passing checks.
  - Phase 1 — the keystone harness, proven end-to-end in CI: task spec → Docker
  build → snapshot → agent → diff captured before tests are injected → JUnit
  F2P/P2P scoring → weighted compose with regression gate. Gold resolves the
  tiny task; noop doesn't.
  - Phase 2 — real agents + orchestration: api-agent (tool-loop over
  Anthropic/OpenAI-compatible → covers Gemini/Kimi), claude-code, aider, and a
  generic command BYO adapter; provider-agnostic LLM client + cost accounting;
  --config, adapter-check, rich progress; entry-point discovery.

  Verified: 32 unit tests + 3 Docker integration tests (gold resolves, noop
  fails, command adapter resolves) — green on GitHub's runners across Python
  3.11/3.12/3.13.

  Operational hygiene along the way: caught and fixed a real critical Dependabot
  alert (trivy-action supply-chain compromise) via the gated PR flow; diagnosed
  the GitHub PR-API 500 incident (not a gh issue, though I did update gh
  2.95→2.96); worked around the broken homebrew Python by running all dev/CI in
  Docker.

  Remaining (issues filed)

  - #3 Phase 3 — dependency-coverage scorer + detector registry, LLM-judge
  rubric, cost/rate-limiting, and the replication + new-feature sample tasks.
  - #4 Phase 4 — HF datasets, report --compare leaderboard, budget/variance,
  hardening, published schema.

  This is a solid checkpoint. Want me to continue into Phase 3 (the
  dependency-coverage scorer and LLM judge are the parts that make reforge
  distinct from SWE-bench), or pause here for you to review the repo first?

      46      return results

      48  
      49 +def parse_go_json(text: str) -> list[TestCaseResult]:          
      50 +    """Parse ``go test -json`` output (one JSON object per line
         +)."""                                                          
      51 +    import json                                                
      52 +                                                               
      53 +    status_map = {"pass": TestStatus.passed, "fail": TestStatus
         +.failed, "skip": TestStatus.skipped}                           
      54 +    latest: dict[str, TestStatus] = {}                         
      55 +    for line in text.splitlines():                             
      56 +        line = line.strip()                                    
      57 +        if not line:                                           
      58 +            continue                                           
      59 +        try:                                                   
      60 +            event = json.loads(line)                           
      61 +        except json.JSONDecodeError:                           
      62 +            continue                                           
      63 +        test = event.get("Test")                               
      64 +        action = event.get("Action")                           
      65 +        if not test or action not in status_map:               
      66 +            continue                                           
      67 +        pkg = event.get("Package", "")                         
      68 +        nodeid = f"{pkg}::{test}" if pkg else test             
      69 +        latest[nodeid] = status_map[action]                    
      70 +    return [TestCaseResult(nodeid, status) for nodeid, status i
         +n latest.items()]                                              
      71 +                                                               
      72 +                                                               
      73  def _status_for(case: ElementTree.Element) -> TestStatus:
      74      if case.find("error") is not None:
      75          return TestStatus.error

      52 +class FakeFsContainer(ContainerHandle):                        
      53 +    """A container backed by an in-memory file tree.           
      54 +                                                               
      55 +    Supports the two commands the dependency scorer issues: ``f
         +ind . -type f`` and                                            
      56 +    ``cat <path>``. Paths in ``files`` are workspace-relative (
         +no leading ./).                                                
      57 +    """                                                        
      58 +                                                               
      59 +    def __init__(self, files: dict[str, str]) -> None:         
      60 +        self.files = files                                     
      61 +                                                               
      62 +    @property                                                  
      63 +    def id(self) -> str:                                       
      64 +        return "fakefs"                                        
      65 +                                                               
      66 +    def exec(                                                  
      67 +        self,                                                  
      68 +        cmd: list[str],                                        
      69 +        *,                                                     
      70 +        workdir: str | None = None,                            
      71 +        env: dict[str, str] | None = None,                     
      72 +        timeout_s: int | None = None,                          
      73 +        stream_to: IO[str] | Path | None = None,               
      74 +    ) -> ExecResult:                                           
      75 +        joined = " ".join(cmd)                                 
      76 +        if "find . -type f" in joined:                         
      77 +            listing = "\n".join(f"./{p}" for p in sorted(self.f
         +iles))                                                         
      78 +            return ExecResult(exit_code=0, output=listing, time
         +d_out=False)                                                   
      79 +        if cmd[0] == "cat":                                    
      80 +            path = cmd[1]                                      
      81 +            if path in self.files:                             
      82 +                return ExecResult(exit_code=0, output=self.file
         +s[path], timed_out=False)                                      
      83 +            return ExecResult(exit_code=1, output="", timed_out
         +=False)                                                        
      84 +        return ExecResult(exit_code=0, output="", timed_out=Fal
         +se)                                                            
      85 +                                                               
      86 +    def copy_in(self, src: Path, dest_dir: str) -> None:       
      87 +        return None                                            
      88 +                                                               
      89 +    def read_file(self, path: str) -> bytes:                   
      90 +        return b""                                             
      91 +                                                               
      92 +    def stop(self) -> None:                                    
      93 +        return None

⏺ Found it. The failure is deterministic on the runner (re-ran, failed again in
  ~1s). The tell: tiny-task (flat src/) passes everywhere, but analytics/staging
  have a nested src/app/ and src/environments/. My copy_in iterates rglob("*")
  and calls tar.add (which is recursive by default), so nested files get added
  twice with directory entries — benign on Docker Desktop, but the runner's
  Docker mishandles it and the files don't land. Let me fix copy_in to add
  top-level entries once.

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

      64 +def _target_parents(patch_text: str) -> list[str]:             
      65 +    """Parent directories of files the patch creates or modifie
         +s (from +++ lines)."""                                         
      66 +    parents: list[str] = []                                    
      67 +    for line in patch_text.splitlines():                       
      68 +        if line.startswith("+++ ") and not line.startswith("+++
         + /dev/null"):                                                  
      69 +            path = line[4:].strip()                            
      70 +            if path.startswith("b/"):                          
      71 +                path = path[2:]                                
      72 +            if "/" in path:                                    
      73 +                parents.append(path.rsplit("/", 1)[0])         
      74 +    return sorted(set(parents))

⏺ Found it. The app directory is owned by 1001:1001 (the GitHub runner's
  checkout user) with mode 755 — everything else is root. copy_in preserves host
  file ownership in the tar, so on the runner the container process can't write
  into it (Docker Desktop silently remaps ownership to root, masking the bug
  locally). The fix: reset ownership to root when building the tar.

⏺ Update(src/reforge/runtime/docker_runtime.py)  ⎿  Added 16 lines
      33  _MAX_CAPTURE_BYTES = 8 * 1024 * 1024
      34  
      35  
      36 +def _root_owned(tarinfo: tarfile.TarInfo) -> tarfile.TarInfo:  
      37 +    """Force copied files to be root-owned in the container.   
      38 +                                                               
      39 +    Host checkouts (e.g. on CI runners) can be owned by a non-r
         +oot uid. Preserving                                            
      40 +    that ownership leaves the workspace unwritable by the conta
         +iner process, so the                                           
      41 +    agent and gold patches fail with confusing ENOENT/EACCES er
         +rors. Docker Desktop                                           
      42 +    hides this by remapping ownership; CI does not. Normalizing
         + to root matches the                                           
      43 +    intent: the workspace belongs to whoever runs inside the co
         +ntainer.                                                       
      44 +    """                                                        
      45 +    tarinfo.uid = 0                                            
      46 +    tarinfo.gid = 0                                            
      47 +    tarinfo.uname = "root"                                     
      48 +    tarinfo.gname = "root"                                     
      49 +    return tarinfo                                             
      50 +                                                               
      51 +                                                               
      52  class DockerContainerHandle(ContainerHandle):
      53      def __init__(self, client: DockerClient, container: Contain
          er) -> None:
      54          self._client = client

⏺ Phase 3 merged — the core product is complete. All four milestones (0–3) are
  shipped to protected main with green CI.

  What just landed (Phase 3)

  - Dependency-coverage scorer — the piece that makes reforge more than a
  test-pass benchmark. Detector registry (python_imports, env_refs,
  terraform_refs, service_deps, grep); reports coverage plus the explicit list 
  of dependencies the agent missed.
  - LLM judge — rubric scoring via the provider-agnostic client, temperature 0,
  N-sample median, anti-reward-hacking prompt, records model + prompt hash,
  never gates. Degrades gracefully to a reweight when no key is present.
  - Two gold-verified sample tasks: newfeature-analytics and
  replication-staging-env, both checked in CI.
  - go test -json parser, list detectors,
  --no-judge/--judge-model/--judge-samples.

  A real cross-platform bug, caught by the gated flow

  The sample tasks failed only on CI, never locally. I traced it (dind repro,
  amd64 repro, workspace listings, an enriched error) to the root cause: copy_in
  preserved host file ownership, so on Linux (where actions/checkout owns files
  as uid 1001) the container process couldn't write into the workspace — Docker
  Desktop had been masking it by remapping ownership to root. This would have
  broken every Linux user, not just CI. Fixed by normalizing copied files to
  root ownership in the tar.

  Status

  - Merged PRs: #1 (trivy hotfix), #5 (Phase 2), #6 (Phase 3). Issues #2, #3
  auto-closed.
  - Remaining: #4 Phase 4 (scale/polish) — HF dataset loader, report --compare
  cross-model leaderboard, --max-cost-usd budget + --repeats variance, security
  hardening, published JSON Schema, task cookbook.

  Phase 4 is the last one and it's polish rather than core capability. Want me
  to continue into it now (I'd prioritize report --compare and cost/variance
  controls, since those directly serve the frontier-vs-non-frontier comparison),
  or pause here so you can review the repo first?

       82 +## Comparing agents and models                                
       83 +                                                              
       84 +The whole point is to compare. Run each candidate, then put th
          +em on one board:                                              
       85 +                                                              
       86 +```bash                                                       
       87 +reforge run --dataset ./tasks --adapter api-agent --model clau
          +de-sonnet-4-6 --run-id claude

          +4.1 --run-id gpt

          +rate, mean score,

          +mes flake:

       98 +  pass doesn't look like a reliable one.

          +d reaches your                                                
      100 +  budget, so a large dataset against a frontier model can't ru
          +n away with the bill.

  ┌───────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ Phase │                               What                               │
  ├───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │       │ Repo bootstrap: Apache 2.0, src-layout, ruff/mypy/pytest, full   │
  │ 0     │ .github/ (CI · CodeQL · Trivy · Dependabot · templates ·         │
  │       │ CODEOWNERS), branch protection, humanized docs + Mermaid         │
  │       │ architecture diagrams                                            │
  ├───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │       │ Keystone harness: task spec → Docker build → snapshot → agent →  │
  │ 1     │ diff captured before tests injected → JUnit F2P/P2P scoring →    │
  │       │ weighted compose with regression gate                            │
  ├───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │       │ Agents: api-agent (Anthropic/OpenAI-compatible tool loop →       │
  │ 2     │ Gemini/Kimi too), claude-code, aider, generic command BYO;       │
  │       │ provider-agnostic LLM client + cost; entry-point discovery;      │
  │       │ adapter-check                                                    │
  ├───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │       │ Dependency-coverage scorer (names what the agent missed) + LLM   │
  │ 3     │ judge (rubric, N-sample median, never gates); two gold-verified  │
  │       │ sample tasks (replication + new-feature); go test parser         │
  ├───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 4     │ report --compare cross-model leaderboard, --repeats variance,    │
  │       │ --max-cost-usd budget, published JSON Schema                     │
  └───────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Verified: 42 unit tests + 4 Docker integration tests, green across Python
  3.11/3.12/3.13 on GitHub's runners. Every shipped task is gold-verified in CI.

  Things worth calling out

  - A real cross-platform bug, caught only because we used the gated CI flow:
  copy_in preserved host file ownership, so on Linux (uid 1001 checkouts) the
  container couldn't write the workspace — Docker Desktop had masked it. That
  would have broken every Linux user. Traced via dind/amd64 repros and fixed by
  normalizing to root ownership.
  - Handled the GitHub PR-API outage mid-session and the broken homebrew Python
  (all dev/CI runs in Docker — saved to memory for next time).
  - Deferred honestly: issue #8 tracks the HF dataset loader and Podman backend
  (they don't fit the containerized task-dir model cleanly yet) rather than
  shipping something low-quality.

  The humanizer skill was applied to all prose (zero em-dashes), and
  subagents/the command adapter validated the full pipeline end-to-end without
  needing API keys.

  The repo is ready to use: reforge run --dataset ./tasks --adapter api-agent 
  --model <frontier>, then --model <non-frontier>, then reforge report --compare
  to see them side by side.

⏺ Context is at 51% — plenty of room. Let me work through issue #8's three
  parts. They each have a sensible, defensible design, so I'll proceed and be
  transparent about what's CI-tested vs. mock-tested:

  1. HF dataset loader — --dataset hf:owner/repo pulls a dataset repo of reforge
  task dirs via huggingface_hub.snapshot_download, then reuses
  load_dataset_dir. (Row-based HF datasets don't map to the task-dir model; a
  git-backed dataset repo does.)
  2. Podman — docker-py already honors DOCKER_HOST, and Podman exposes a
  Docker-compatible API socket, so the tested DockerRuntime code path works
  against it. I'll make --runtime podman a first-class switch that points at the
  podman socket, rather than shipping an untested parallel backend.
  3. disk_quota — enforce via --storage-opt size= best-effort, degrading with a
  warning when the storage driver doesn't support it.

  Let me set up tasks and a branch.

⏺ Issue #8 is done — merged, closed, and no open issues remain.

  What shipped (PR #9)

  - HF dataset loader — reforge run --dataset hf:owner/repo[@rev] pulls a
  git-backed dataset repo of reforge task directories via
  huggingface_hub.snapshot_download (reforge[hf] extra) and runs it like any
  local dataset. I deliberately went with the dataset-repo-of-task-dirs
  convention rather than trying to shoehorn row-based HF datasets into the
  containerized model.
  - Podman runtime — --runtime docker|podman. Podman exposes a Docker-compatible
  API socket, so this points the already-tested DockerRuntime code path at
  Podman's socket (rootless default, DOCKER_HOST-overridable) via a new runtime
  factory — not a separate unverified backend. The base_url plumbing is verified
  against a live daemon.
  - disk_quota — resources.disk_quota wired to --storage-opt size=, best-effort:
  it retries without the quota and warns when the storage driver can't support
  it, rather than failing the run.

  on GitHub's runners; ruff/mypy clean; docs updated (README datasets/runtimes

  and a full feature set: hybrid scoring with the distinctive

❯ is this ready for alpha? if not? what do we need to get there?                
⏺ Let me be honest rather than just cheerlead — let me check the one thing that
  most affects alpha readiness: what happens when a real agent/adapter throws
  (e.g., an API error), since that path has never run live.

⏺ Bash(cd /Users/filip/Documents/Personal/reforge
      echo "=== task_runner exception boundary ==="; grep -n
      "except\|adapter.run\|adapter.validate\|raise" src/reforge/run…)
  ⎿  === task_runner exception boundary ===
     45:    """Execute a single task and return its result. Never raises fo
     r task-level
     … +10 lines (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Confirmed a real gap. Honest answer: the core is alpha-solid, but I wouldn't 
  call the whole tool alpha-ready yet — for two specific reasons.

  What's genuinely solid

  - The harness (build → isolate → snapshot → capture diff → inject verifier →
  score) is proven end-to-end in CI, including the cross-platform ownership bug
  fix.
  - Scoring (tests + dependency-coverage + judge composition) is real and
  unit-tested.
  - Deterministic adapters (gold, noop, command) work against Docker on Linux
  CI.
  - Packaging, release, protected CI, docs, security posture (network-off,
  cap-drop) are all in place.

  The two things that block an honest "alpha ready"

  1. The marquee feature has never run against a real model. api-agent, the
  judge, claude-code, and aider are unit-tested with fake clients only. The
  whole pitch is "benchmark frontier vs non-frontier on your code," and not one
  real API call has gone through the actual Anthropic/OpenAI SDK path. Until
  that's exercised once, "it works" is an assumption, not a fact.

  2. A real robustness bug: a provider error crashes the entire run. run_task
  catches only ReforgeError (task_runner.py:160). Every real provider exception
  — anthropic.APIError, openai.RateLimitError, a dropped connection — is not a
  ReforgeError, so it propagates out, future.result() re-raises in the
  orchestrator, and the whole benchmark dies, losing every other result. At the
  scale reforge is meant for (many tasks × models), transient 429/500s are
  guaranteed, so this would bite on day one.

  What I'd do to get to alpha

  ┌──────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ Priority │                             Item                              │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │          │ Wrap adapter.validate/run + scorers so any exception becomes  │
  │ Must     │ a failed TaskResult, never a run crash. (I can do + test      │
  │          │ now.)                                                         │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Must     │ One live smoke test: api-agent + judge against a real model,  │
  │          │ end to end. Needs an API key — you run it, or hand me one.    │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Should   │ Retry/backoff for 429/500 in the LLM client.                  │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │          │ api-agent: graceful handling when a model never calls         │
  │ Should   │ finish() (currently just hits max_steps, which is OK) and     │
  │          │ note unbounded context growth on long tasks.                  │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │          │ CHANGELOG/known-limitations section, a couple more realistic  │
  │ Nice     │ sample tasks, mark the pricing table as approximate, add a    │
  │          │ CLAUDE.md.                                                    │
  └──────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  My recommendation: let me implement the two Must code fixes plus Should
  retry/backoff now (all testable without a key), then you run a real-key smoke
  test (or provide a key and I will). After that it's honestly alpha.

  Want me to make those fixes as one PR?

      27 +T = TypeVar("T")                                               
      28  
      29 +_RETRYABLE_HINTS = (                                           
      30 +    "rate limit",                                              
      31 +    "429",                                                     
      32 +    "timeout",                                                 
      33 +    "temporarily",                                             
      34 +    "overloaded",                                              
      35 +    "connection",                                              
      36 +    "500",                                                     
      37 +    "502",                                                     
      38 +    "503",                                                     
      39 +    "504",                                                     
      40 +)                                                              
      41 +                                                               
      42 +                                                               
      43 +def _is_retryable(exc: Exception) -> bool:                     
      44 +    status = getattr(exc, "status_code", None)                 
      45 +    if isinstance(status, int) and (status == 429 or 500 <= sta
         +tus < 600):                                                    
      46 +        return True                                            
      47 +    msg = str(exc).lower()                                     
      48 +    return any(hint in msg for hint in _RETRYABLE_HINTS)       
      49 +                                                               
      50 +                                                               
      51 +def call_with_retries(                                         
      52 +    func: Callable[[], T],                                     
      53 +    *,                                                         
      54 +    retries: int = 3,                                          
      55 +    base_delay: float = 1.0,                                   
      56 +    sleep: Callable[[float], None] = time.sleep,               
      57 +) -> T:                                                        
      58 +    """Call ``func``, retrying transient provider errors with e
         +xponential backoff."""                                         
      59 +    for attempt in range(retries + 1):                         
      60 +        try:                                                   
      61 +            return func()                                      
      62 +        except Exception as exc:                               
      63 +            if attempt >= retries or not _is_retryable(exc):   
      64 +                raise                                          
      65 +            sleep(base_delay * (2**attempt))                   
      66 +    raise AssertionError("unreachable")  # pragma: no cover    
      67 +                                                               
      68 +                                                               
      69  @dataclass
      70  class LLMUsage:
      71      input_tokens: int = 0

⏺ Merged. Here's the honest verdict.

  Is it alpha-ready now? Effectively yes — with one thing only you can finish.

  Everything I flagged as blocking is done and merged (PR #10):

  - The run-killer bug is fixed and proven. A provider error (429/5xx/dropped
  connection), a scorer bug, or an adapter crash now fails only its own task,
  never the whole run. There's a Docker integration test that raises inside an
  adapter and asserts the run survives with a labeled task error.
  - The api-agent's in-container tool loop is now actually exercised — a real
  container, real base64 file writes, resolving the tiny task end to end (with a
  fake client, so no key needed). That was the biggest untested chunk of the
  marquee feature, and it works.
  - Transient errors retry with backoff in the LLM client (unit-tested).
  - Polish: CHANGELOG, README "Known limitations", pricing table marked
  approximate, CLAUDE.md for future sessions.

  The one remaining step (needs your key)

  The literal Anthropic/OpenAI HTTP call still hasn't run — I have no API key in
  this environment. I turned that into an automated, key-gated live smoke test
  (test_live_api_agent_and_judge) that runs the real SDK path (agent solving a
  task + judge scoring it) and skips without a key. Run it once:

  ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-... docker run --rm \
    -v "$PWD":/repo -w /repo -v reforge-pip-cache:/root/.cache/pip \
    -v /var/run/docker.sock:/var/run/docker.sock \
    -e ANTHROPIC_API_KEY python:3.12-slim bash -c \
    "pip install -q -e '.[dev,judge]' && pytest -m docker -k live -q"

  If that passes, the core promise is verified against a real model and I'd call
  it honestly alpha. (You could also drop ANTHROPIC_API_KEY into GitHub Actions
  secrets so CI runs it.)

  One optional follow-up

  The published v0.1.0 release predates all this hardening. When you're ready,
  cutting v0.1.1 (git tag -a v0.1.1 -m ... && git push origin v0.1.1) would ship
  the failure-isolation, retries, HF/Podman, and compare/cost features in a
  release. Say the word and I'll tag it.

❯ /plan                                                                         
  ⎿  Current Plan
     /Users/filip/.claude/plans/idempotent-moseying-lemur.md

     reforge — AI Coding-Agent Benchmark for Your Own Codebase

     Context

     Companies and organizations must decide which AI coding agent/model to adopt
     — frontier tools
     (Claude, GPT) or non-frontier ones (Gemini, Kimi, etc.). That decision is
     hard because the honest
     question isn't "how does model X score on public benchmarks" but "how well
     does agent X do work
     against our own codebase." Two capabilities matter most:

     1. Replication — reproduce existing features/environments/applications using
     context already in
     the codebase (e.g. "create staging + test environments mirroring prod/dev":
     did it catch all
     dependencies? how accurately?).
     2. New-feature implementation — build net-new functionality with little/no
     prior context
     (e.g. "add an analytics dashboard from internal events data").

     reforge is a self-hosted, Docker-based, model-agnostic CLI (no UI, pure CLI
     + library, Python)
     that runs a company's own tasks against any agent and produces reproducible,
     comparable scores. It
     mirrors proven patterns from SWE-bench (task = repo@commit + problem + gold
     patch +
     FAIL_TO_PASS/PASS_TO_PASS, containerized harness, gold self-verification,
     results JSON) and
     Terminal-bench (task-bundle-as-directory with Dockerfile + verifier +
     oracle; strict
     agent/harness separation), and adds two things the problem needs that
     neither has: a
     dependency-coverage scorer and a unified two-category task schema.

     Locked decisions: Python · BYO-agent adapter interface (+ reference
     adapters) · hybrid scoring
     (deterministic tests + dependency-coverage + LLM-judge rubric) · both task
     categories in v1 ·
     self-hosted, Apache 2.0 · GitHub with protected main, Dependabot,
     CodeQL/Trivy, CI gates
     (patterned on the Burnwise/burnwise repo).

     Reference patterns reused (do not reinvent)

     - SWE-bench containerized eval + F2P/P2P resolved criterion + gold-patch
     self-verification.
     - Terminal-bench task bundle (dir with Dockerfile + verifier + oracle) +
     agent/adapter split.
     - Burnwise GitHub setup
     (/Users/filip/Documents/Personal/Burnwise/burnwise/.github/): reuse its
     codeql.yml, security.yml (Trivy SARIF + fixable CRITICAL/HIGH gate),
     dependabot.yml
     (npm→pip, docker, github-actions), issue/PR templates, config.yml,
     SECURITY.md, release.yml,
     CONTRIBUTING.md, CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md (Contributor Covenant 2.1), Makefile +
     .gitignore style.
     Adapt Node→Python (ruff/mypy/pytest instead of eslint/tsc/playwright).

     Package layout (src/ layout, package reforge, CLI reforge)

     reforge/
     ├── pyproject.toml (hatchling, PEP 621, entry points) · LICENSE (Apache 2.0)
     · NOTICE · README.md
     ├── CONTRIBUTING.md · CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md · SECURITY.md · Makefile ·
     .gitignore · .pre-commit-config.yaml
     ├── .github/{workflows/{ci.yml,codeql.yml,security.yml,release.yml},dependab
     ot.yml,
     │
     ISSUE_TEMPLATE/*,pull_request_template.md,config.yml,CODEOWNERS}
     ├── docs/ (architecture.md + Mermaid diagrams, task-authoring.md,
     adapter-authoring.md)
     ├── src/reforge/
     │   ├── cli.py            # Typer app, thin — delegates to services
     │   ├── config.py         # pydantic-settings: registries, dirs, secrets
     │   ├── spec/             # models.py (pydantic v2 TaskSpec), loader.py,
     validate.py, schema.py (JSON Schema export)
     │   ├── dataset/          # loader.py (local dir + HF), registry.py
     │   ├── workspace/        # source.py (git@sha|local|tarball via subprocess
     git), prepare.py (copy, strip_git, hidden_paths)
     │   ├── runtime/          # base.py (ContainerRuntime ABC +
     ContainerHandle), docker_runtime.py, image.py, limits.py
     │   ├── adapters/         # base.py (AgentAdapter ABC), registry.py (entry
     points), process.py, claude_code.py, aider.py, api_agent.py, noop.py,
     gold.py
     │   ├── runner/           # orchestrator.py (ThreadPoolExecutor),
     task_runner.py (per-task lifecycle), artifacts.py, run_context.py
     │   ├── scoring/          # base.py (Scorer ABC), tests.py, log_parsers.py,
     dependency.py, judge.py, compose.py
     │   ├── report/           # models.py, aggregate.py, render.py
     (rich/markdown/json)
     │   ├── llm/              # client.py (provider-agnostic + rate limiter),
     cost.py (pricing table)
     │   └── utils/            # logging.py (structlog), hashing.py, errors.py
     ├── tasks/                # sample-replication-*, sample-newfeature-*
     (shipped dataset)
     └── tests/{unit,integration,fixtures/tiny-task}

     Task specification (on-disk task directory, Terminal-bench style)

     tasks/<id>/
     ├── task.yaml        # TaskSpec (pydantic-validated)
     ├── Dockerfile       # environment (pin base image by digest)
     ├── verifier/        # run_tests.sh + held-out tests — injected AFTER diff
     capture
     ├── gold/solution.patch  # oracle for self-verification
     └── rubric.yaml      # optional (or inline in task.yaml)
     Unified schema (single category: replication | new_feature field), key
     blocks:
     - source (git repo@SHA | local | tarball; strip_git; subdir)
     - environment (dockerfile, build_args, workdir)
     - instruction (the prompt) · context (visible/hidden globs)
     - verification (entrypoint, framework, fail_to_pass, pass_to_pass, timeout)
     - dependency_coverage (required.{services,config_refs,imports} ground truth
     + detectors[])
     - rubric (weighted criteria, scale, judge prompts)
     - scoring.weights {tests, dependency_coverage, judge} +
     gate.require_pass_to_pass
     - resources (cpus, memory, pids, network=none default, timeouts, disk_quota)

     Replication tasks lean on dependency_coverage.required + rubric; new-feature
     tasks lean on
     fail_to_pass tests + rubric. Ship one worked example of each in tasks/.

     Agent adapter contract (adapters/base.py)

     Thin, I/O-oriented; harness owns the container, adapter drives its agent
     inside it.
     - AdapterInput(instruction, workspace_path, container: ContainerHandle, 
     model, config, env, logger, timeout_s)
     - AdapterResult(success, trace_path, token_usage, cost_usd, exit_code, 
     metadata, error)
     - AgentAdapter.validate(input) (fail fast) + run(input) (modify files in
     /workspace, produce trace).
     - Harness captures the diff (git diff vs a pre-agent commit inside the
     container) — uniform across
     all adapters; adapter never computes scores or reports the diff.
     - Discovery via entry points ([project.entry-points."reforge.adapters"]) →
     third-party pip install.
     - Reference adapters: noop + gold (no LLM, for testing), api_agent
     (Anthropic/OpenAI/Gemini
     mini-loop), claude-code, aider.

     Per-task lifecycle (runner/task_runner.py, SWE-bench-style)

     resolve source → prep workspace (strip_git, hidden_paths; source only, no 
     tests/gold) → build/pull
     image (cache key = hash(Dockerfile+args), serialized builds) → start
     container (resource limits,
     --network none, --cap-drop ALL, no docker socket) → snapshot pre-agent
     commit → adapter.run
     (agent_timeout, SIGKILL) → capture prediction.patch → inject verifier + 
     held-out tests → run
     scorers → compose → persist
     runs/<run-id>/tasks/<id>/{prediction.patch,test.log,dep_report.json, 
     judge.json,result.json,agent_trace.log} → cleanup container. Orchestrator =
     bounded
     ThreadPoolExecutor (--concurrency), image-build lock, API rate-limiter,
     run.json provenance
     (tool/adapter versions, image digests, source SHA).

     Hybrid scoring (scoring/)

     - tests.py — exec verifier, parse machine formats (pytest junitxml, jest
     --json, go test -json,
     JUnit XML) → F2P = fraction of fail_to_pass now passing; P2P boolean;
     resolved = F2P==1 ∧ P2P.
     - dependency.py — detectors (python_imports via AST, env_refs,
     terraform_refs,
     service_deps from compose/k8s, grep fallback; registry-extensible) →
     coverage = |required∩found|/|required|,
     and an explicit missed list (the most actionable buyer output).
     - judge.py — structured-JSON rubric scoring, temperature 0, --judge-samples
     median, anti-reward-hacking
     prompt, records model id + prompt hash; weighted, never hard-gates.
     - compose.py — final = Σ weight·subscore; regressions (P2P fail) → final = 
     0; report both
     continuous final_score and binary resolved. Per-task result.json + aggregate
     leaderboard
     (resolved_rate, mean_final_score, by_category, mean_dep_coverage, cost,
     duration).

     CLI surface (Typer)

     reforge init <id> [--category] · reforge validate <dir|--dataset> ·
     reforge verify-gold <dir> (apply gold, assert resolved — CI gate) ·
     reforge run --dataset --adapter --model [--run-id --concurrency --task-id 
     --judge-model --judge-samples --no-judge --network --max-cost-usd --repeats 
     --no-cache --dry-run] ·
     reforge report <run-dir> [--format table|markdown|json --leaderboard 
     --compare RUN...] ·
     reforge list adapters|datasets|detectors · reforge adapter-check --adapter 
     --model.

     Dependencies (lean; LLM/HF isolated as extras)

     Core: pydantic+pydantic-settings, typer, rich, docker (SDK), pyyaml,
     structlog; git via
     subprocess (no GitPython). Extras:
     reforge[judge]→anthropic,openai,google-genai;
     reforge[hf]→datasets. Dev: pytest+pytest-mock, ruff, mypy, hatchling.

     GitHub / CI / security (self-hosted, protected main)

     - Branch protection on main: require PR + passing checks (ci, codeql,
     security), no direct pushes,
     linear history; CODEOWNERS.
     - ci.yml: ruff → mypy → unit tests → Docker job (gold self-verify +
     noop/gold adapters); LLM-judge
     mocked (no API cost in CI). codeql.yml (python, weekly). security.yml (Trivy
     SARIF + fixable
     CRITICAL/HIGH gate). release.yml (tag→GitHub release, validate
     tag==version). dependabot.yml
     (pip + github-actions, weekly, grouped). Issue/PR templates, config.yml,
     SECURITY.md,
     CONTRIBUTING.md, CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md, .pre-commit-config.yaml.
     - Create GitHub issues per phase item below; do work on feature branches →
     PRs into main.

     Security / risk mitigations

     Untrusted agent output: one container/task, --network none default,
     --cap-drop ALL, pids/mem/cpu
     limits, disk quota, hard timeouts, workspace is a copy, no docker-socket
     mount — document
     "run on disposable/isolated hosts." Reward hacking: tests/gold injected
     after diff capture; P2P
     regression gate; judge detects hardcoding; optional held-out-test hash
     check. Reproducibility: pin
     SHAs + image digests + versions in run.json; --no-judge for fully
     deterministic runs.
     Cost: per-task/run accounting + --max-cost-usd abort. Flakiness: --repeats +
     variance in report.

     Phased delivery (→ GitHub issues, feature-branch PRs)

     - Phase 0 — Bootstrap: repo, pyproject/src-layout, Apache 2.0
     LICENSE+NOTICE, ruff/mypy/pytest,
     full .github/ (CI/CodeQL/Trivy/Dependabot/templates), branch protection,
     README vision, docs skeleton.
     - Phase 1 — Keystone vertical slice (must be real end-to-end): spec
     (new_feature, local source),
     docker_runtime, task_runner, tests scorer (junitxml), compose (tests-only),
     noop+gold adapters,
     validate/verify-gold/run --task-id/report --json, tests/fixtures/tiny-task.
     Exit:
     verify-gold passes and noop fails on tiny-task, in CI with Docker.
     - Phase 2 — Real agents + orchestration: full adapter contract + entry-point
     registry + process.py;
     api_agent, claude-code, aider; orchestrator (concurrency, build lock,
     provenance); git source +
     strip_git; run --dataset, list adapters, adapter-check; rich progress +
     table/markdown reports.
     - Phase 3 — Full hybrid scoring + replication: dependency scorer + detector
     registry; judge harness
       - sampling/median + cost accounting + rate limiter; full compose/gating +
     schemas; replication category
     end-to-end + sample replication task; more log parsers (jest, go test,
     JUnit).
     - Phase 4 — Scale/hardening: HF dataset loader ([hf]); report --compare
     leaderboard;
     --max-cost-usd/--repeats/variance; security hardening pass; docs site +
     architecture diagrams +
     authoring guides + published JSON Schema.
     - Phase 5 — Ecosystem: company-agnostic sample task cookbook; third-party
     adapter plugin template;
     public leaderboard format.

     Subagents as stand-in test-subject LLMs (development-time)

     To exercise the full reforge run pipeline live during Phases 1–2 without 
     needing external agent
     binaries or API keys, use spawned Claude Code subagents as the "agent under
     test":
     - Add a dev/reference adapter (e.g. adapters/subagent.py, or drive the
     existing api_agent via a
     subagent) that receives the task instruction + workspace and returns file
     edits — letting me run a
     real end-to-end benchmark against a live LLM through the harness while
     building it.
     - Use subagents to author and stress-test the sample tasks (generate
     candidate solutions, confirm
     fail_to_pass really fails without the fix and passes with it, sanity-check
     rubric wording).
     - This validates orchestration, diff capture, verifier injection, and all
     three scorers end-to-end
     before wiring real frontier/non-frontier adapters. (Note: the subagent
     adapter is a development/test
     convenience, not a shipped product dependency — the shipped path is the
     entry-point adapters.)

     Documentation authored with the humanizer skill

     Run every human-facing doc through the humanizer skill so it doesn't read as
     AI-generated:
     README, CONTRIBUTING, CODE_OF_CONDUCT, SECURITY, docs/architecture.md,
     docs/task-authoring.md,
     docs/adapter-authoring.md, and issue/PR templates. Apply after drafting each
     doc, before commit.
     Architecture diagrams (Mermaid) accompany the humanized prose.

     Verification

     - Unit: spec round-trips + JSON Schema; log parsers on captured fixtures;
     dependency detectors;
     compose math + regression gating; judge normalization (mocked LLM);
     registry/limits/aggregate.
     - Integration (Docker-marked): gold-patch self-verification on tiny-task and
     every shipped task
     (resolved == true); noop adapter → resolved == false, F2P==0; concurrency
     isolation smoke.
     - Live E2E via subagents: run reforge run on the sample tasks using a
     subagent-backed adapter
     as the test-subject LLM — confirms the full pipeline and produces a real
     leaderboard row.
     - Manual E2E: reforge run --dataset ./tasks --adapter api_agent --model 
     <frontier> --run-id smoke
     then --model <non-frontier>, and reforge report --compare to confirm
     cross-model leaderboard.
     - CI green on ruff+mypy+unit+Docker gold-verify before any merge to main.
